      "creator_type": "member",
      "description": "Ensure the integrity of software updates during delivery.\n- Implement mechanisms (checksums, digital signatures/certificates) within the update process to verify code authenticity and integrity.\n- Deliver security updates in a secure manner to prevent tampering during transit.",
      "due_date": null,
      "id": "031de248-83fc-4301-a791-70d39030f8f1",

      "creator_type": "member",
      "description": "Establish processes and tools to protect code integrity throughout the lifecycle.\n- Enforce code control procedures (check-in/check-out, strict access controls).\n- Protect integrity and confidentiality of software code, third-party components, libraries, and configuration files.\n- Ensure timely detection and investigation of unauthorized attempts to access or tamper with code.",
      "due_date": null,
      "id": "da74b704-1a56-4677-a4ba-da480a37f68d",
      "identifier": "RGT-477",
      "labels": [

      "title": "Governance: Software Code Integrity Maintenance (Objective 6.1)",

    {

      "creator_id": "a38f4c8f-f0f7-4235-9a22-6e8194317885",
      "creator_type": "member",
      "description": "Establish a formal system for uniquely identifying and tracking each software version.\n- Define a formal system or methodology for uniquely identifying software versions (e.g. semantic versioning) in a logical, sequential manner.\n- Ensure all changes to software functionality are clearly associated with a unique software version.\n- Track versioning of all relevant software components associated with each release.",
      "due_date": null,
      "id": "44f0d6f0-7d71-4134-b9a1-19eb30818f4f",
      "identifier": "RGT-476",
      "labels": [

    {

      "creator_type": "member",
      "description": "Assess, approve, and track all software changes.\n- Implement a change management process requiring analysis of security impact for all software changes.\n- Record all change-management decisions and justifications.\n- Maintain a detailed change inventory showing the creator of the code and the authorizer of the change for every modification.",
      "due_date": null,
      "id": "7ee3c4af-d972-4a06-a06b-6ff35ab03f52",
      "identifier": "RGT-475",
      "labels": [

      "updated_at": "2026-06-24T16:18:50Z",

    {
      "assignee_id": null,
      "assignee_type": null,

      "creator_id": "a38f4c8f-f0f7-4235-9a22-6e8194317885",
      "creator_type": "member",
      "description": "Remediate vulnerabilities in a timely manner and prevent reintroduction.\n- Deploy security fixes in production code according to defined vulnerability severity/criticality criteria.\n- Establish processes (e.g., automated regression tests, secure CI/CD) to prevent previously resolved or similar vulnerabilities from being reintroduced.\n- Justify and obtain management approval for any decisions to not provide security fixes on a case-by-case basis.",
      "due_date": null,
      "id": "bd5bfcd6-c4ae-4136-acce-0d22759f5413",
      "identifier": "RGT-474",
      "labels": [
        {

      "status": "todo",
      "title": "Governance: Vulnerability Remediation and Prevention of Reintroduction (Objective 4.2)",
      "updated_at": "2026-06-24T16:18:50Z",
      "workspace_id": "8a622480-5997-4491-9ff5-17e4a602aab5"
    },
    {
      "assignee_id": null,
      "assignee_type": null,
      "created_at": "2026-06-24T16:12:02Z",
      "creator_id": "a38f4c8f-f0f7-4235-9a22-6e8194317885",
      "creator_type": "member",
      "description": "Implement a mature security testing process to detect existing and emerging vulnerabilities.\n- Conduct security testing (using appropriate static/dynamic/library scanners) throughout the lifecycle, including pre-commit, pre-release, and post-release.\n- Cover the entire codebase, including third-party and open-source libraries.\n- Ensure testing is performed by authorized, objective, and skilled personnel or third parties.\n- Maintain an inventory of vulnerabilities and retain testing details (tools, configs, tests).",
      "due_date": null,
      "id": "bed0f00a-d45a-49c5-aece-a6ff28e6797c",
      "identifier": "RGT-473",
      "labels": [
        {

      "title": "Governance: Security Testing and Vulnerability Detection (Objective 4.1)",
      "updated_at": "2026-06-24T16:18:50Z",

    {

      "creator_type": "member",
      "description": "Monitor, test, and update/replace weak or ineffective software security controls.\n- Implement a process to identify weak or ineffective software security controls (e.g., SSL vs TLS) throughout the software lifecycle.\n- Define and justify criteria for determining control weakness.\n- Update, augment, or replace weak or ineffective security controls in a timely manner.",
      "due_date": null,
      "id": "12e98822-0ae0-4a7b-925f-3e253557c143",
      "identifier": "RGT-472",
      "labels": [

    {
      "assignee_id": null,
      "assignee_type": null,

      "creator_type": "member",
      "description": "Implement software security controls to mitigate identified threats and design flaws.\n- Establish a process to define software-specific security requirements and implement controls (e.g. MFA, logging, parameterized queries) based on threats and architecture.\n- Record, justify, and approve decisions on whether and how to mitigate specific threats/flaws.\n- Record, justify, and approve any remaining residual risk.",
      "due_date": null,
      "id": "69314f44-bd51-463b-9174-4fb903a55d86",
      "identifier": "RGT-471",
      "labels": [

    {
      "assignee_id": null,
      "assignee_type": null,

      "creator_id": "a38f4c8f-f0f7-4235-9a22-6e8194317885",
      "creator_type": "member",
      "description": "Continuously identify and assess threats and design flaws.\n- Establish a process to identify, assess, and monitor threats and flaws, accounting for all inputs/outputs, process/data flows, trust boundaries, and decision points.\n- Maintain a recorded inventory of threats and design flaws, updating it routinely to reflect software changes or emerging threats.\n- Manage open-source components: maintain an inventory, monitor vulnerabilities, define a patching strategy, and update/replace vulnerable components.",
      "due_date": null,
      "id": "4888c9d2-00f4-4e41-b8cf-5f0eabbd9087",
      "identifier": "RGT-470",
      "labels": [

      "creator_type": "member",
      "description": "Identify and classify critical assets for the software products.\n- Establish a mature process (e.g. Mission Impact Analysis) to identify and classify critical assets.\n- Define criteria for identifying critical assets and specifying confidentiality, integrity, and resiliency requirements.\n- Account for all critical asset types: sensitive data, sensitive resources, and sensitive functions.\n- Maintain an inventory of critical assets used by the software.",
      "due_date": null,

      "identifier": "RGT-469",

      "description": "Detect, evaluate, and update/replace weak or ineffective security assurance processes.\n- Implement a process to monitor and detect weak assurance processes (e.g., using metrics/trends, process reviews).\n- Define and justify criteria for determining when a process is weak or ineffective.\n- Update, augment, or replace ineffective processes in a timely manner (e.g., moving from manual to automated scanning as the codebase grows).",
      "due_date": null,

      "description": "Implement and maintain software security assurance processes throughout the software lifecycle.\n- Define and implement security checkpoints (e.g. design reviews, automated scanning, testing, change management) across all lifecycle phases.\n- Maintain a central inventory of all software security assurance processes.\n- Ensure processes address policy rules, align with strategy, have assigned responsibilities, and monitor outcomes.",

      "creator_type": "member",
      "description": "Establish and maintain a formal software security strategy.\n- Define a high-level roadmap or strategy to satisfy the software security policy across the entire product lifecycle.\n- Align the strategy with industry-accepted methodologies (e.g. ISO/IEC 27034, BSIMM, OWASP SAMM, NIST SP 800-160).\n- Review and update the strategy at least annually to account for business changes, external drivers, and product evolution.\n- Communicate the strategy to software development personnel.",
      "due_date": null,
      "id": "6d5942f8-7175-4a52-a921-c127af613c58",
      "identifier": "RGT-465",
      "labels": [

      "description": "Manage and maintain software security skills for development personnel.\n- Establish a mature process for defining, managing, and maintaining software security skills for each role (e.g. architects, developers, testers).\n- Define criteria for maintaining skills, including vendor-provided training, certifications, or regional user groups.\n- Perform a review at least annually to ensure development personnel maintain the necessary skills.\n- Maintain records of training, certifications, or qualification assessments.",

      "description": "Enforce rotation of the Data Encryption Key (DEK) based on operational limits to prevent cryptographic material exhaustion.       "description": "Design and implement a key hierarchy using HKDF-SHA512 to derive distinct, domain-separated keys from the Root KEK for different cryptographic purposes (e.g. vault-wrap, vault-metadata, vault-audit, vault-export). This prevents cross-purpose key reuse.",

    {
      "assignee_id": null,
      "assignee_type": null,
      "created_at": "2026-06-17T05:54:55Z",
      "creator_id": "a38f4c8f-f0f7-4235-9a22-6e8194317885",
      "creator_type": "member",
      "description": "Merged from RGT-17 and RGT-18 which cover complementary research:\n\n- RGT-17: Investigate memoryview-only callback ergonomics for partial zeroization\n- RGT-18: Evaluate mutable-buffer master secret representation\n\nBoth address the same underlying problem: Python cannot guarantee plaintext copies (str, bytes) are unrecoverable. The two research tracks are complementary — partial zeroization (memoryview-only APIs) reduces how much plaintext exists in immutable form; mutable-buffer representation (for MasterSecret) gives a wipeable home for the canonical secret copy.\n\nGoal: design doc + (optionally) a SecureBuffer utility used by both paths. The two tracks converge on a single utility class.\n\nAcceptance:\n  - Design doc covering both approaches + their interaction\n  - (Optional) SecureBuffer utility with ctypes/mmap-backed storage\n  - Threat-model cost/benefit: what attacks does this actually mitigate?\n\nEstimated effort: design + utility ~1-2 weeks.\n\nDepends on: none. Safe to research immediately.\n\nSupersedes: RGT-17, RGT-18.",
      "due_date": null,
      "id": "7954d204-8ca1-4798-a873-1f35a4eae058",

      "creator_type": "member",
      "description": "Transition from simple agent strings to structured identities (ID, Namespace, Purpose). Ensure every request identifies 'Who is asking' and 'Why', allowing for granular ABAC enforcement. This prevents simple malware from impersonating trusted agents.\n\n---\n\nAcceptance: agent identity is a structured object (ID, Namespace, Purpose) not a free-form string. ABAC rules can match on any field. Identity claim verified on every request.\n",

      "number": 25,
      "parent_issue_id": "02c5c5c7-53e0-45e7-9b16-454f6b2293a3",
      "position": -16,

      "start_date": null,
      "status": "done",
      "title": "Implement Formal Agent Identity \u0026 Namespace (Struggle #2)",
      "updated_at": "2026-06-17T15:43:43Z",
      "workspace_id": "8a622480-5997-4491-9ff5-17e4a602aab5"
    },
    {
      "assignee_id": null,
      "assignee_type": null,
      "created_at": "2026-06-17T05:49:52Z",
      "creator_id": "a38f4c8f-f0f7-4235-9a22-6e8194317885",
      "creator_type": "member",
      "description": "## Status 2026-06-17 — SQLite Storage: Add foreign key constraints to audit_logs (deferred)\n\n## Decision 2026-06-17 — defer the FK migration\n\nThe audit chain's tamper-evidence is the integrity guarantee for the audit log. The SQL FK constraint is a belt-and-suspenders addition; the marginal value is small relative to the migration risk (it touches every audit row to add `secret_id` + `namespace` columns, backfill from `secrets`, then add the FK). The current tests already cover the cases that matter: tamper detection, atomicity under concurrency, full-log walk, etc.\n\n**Why deferred:**\n1. The audit chain's prev_hash / entry_hash already detects edits and    inserts. A SQL FK on `audit_logs.secret_id → secrets.secret_id` would    only add a second-line check that the chain doesn't already cover.\n2. The migration requires backfilling `secret_id` and `namespace` on every    existing audit row. For a vault with 100k+ audit rows this is a    non-trivial time. For an empty vault it's a no-op. The risk of a    backfill that fails partway is real.\n3. The FK would be `ON DELETE SET NULL` (audit rows survive secret    deletion), but that means the FK is informational, not a hard    constraint. The chain is the real constraint.\n\n**When to revisit:** if the operator wants defense-in-depth (e.g. a compliance regime that requires SQL-level FK for audit logs), implement as:\n  1. Add `audit_logs.secret_id` and `audit_logs.namespace` columns      (nullable, both).\n  2. Backfill: `UPDATE audit_logs SET secret_id = (SELECT secret_id      FROM secrets WHERE secrets.namespace = audit_logs.namespace      AND secrets.name = audit_logs.secret_name LIMIT 1) WHERE      secret_name IS NOT NULL;`\n  3. Add `FOREIGN KEY (secret_id) REFERENCES secrets(secret_id) ON      DELETE SET NULL`.\n  4. The vault's set_secret, lease_secret, and revoke_secret code      paths need to thread `secret_id` and `namespace` into the      `_append_audit_in_tx` calls. The current code only passes      `secret_name` (a string).\n  5. Add a regression test that confirms the FK rejects an audit row      referencing a non-existent secret_id.\n\nClosing this as 'cancelled' for now; reopen with a specific compliance-or-defense-in-depth requirement when the operator wants it. The audit chain's tamper-evidence is the primary guarantee and is well-pinned by tests.\n\n\n---\n\n## Original description\n\nAdd foreign key constraints and ensure referential integrity across the 'secrets' and 'audit_logs' tables in SQLite.\n\n---\n\nAcceptance: PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON in all connection pools. FK constraints on secrets\u003c-\u003eleases, audit_logs\u003c-\u003esecrets, audit_logs\u003c-\u003eleases. Migration atomicity helper handles partial-failure rollback. Test: deleting secret with audit_logs fails (FK constraint).\n",
      "due_date": null,
      "id": "8dd58c84-ade3-4370-929c-0b4c563f2f7b",

      "description": "Audit log contains noise like debug reads. Debug reads (fingerprint, validation) logged at debug-level, NOT as audit events.\n",

      "description": "Implement a policy layer that scores the risk of requested actions and verifies intent against historical patterns. High-risk actions (e.g., 'Delete Repo') can require explicit human-in-the-loop approval or be blocked for certain agent tiers.\n\n---\n\nAcceptance: risk scoring returns 0-100 per action. Threshold-based blocking configurable. High-risk actions (e.g. delete_repo) require explicit human-in-the-loop approval or are blocked for non-admin agents.\n",

      "description": "From ROADMAP.md 'Under consideration'.\n\nGoal: enable restoring a vault from a backup taken on a different host, without exposing plaintext in transit.\n\nApproach options:\n  - Envelope encryption: re-wrap DEKs under a transport public key, decrypt with a transport private key held only at restore time\n  - Split-key reconstruction: require K-of-N key holders to combine shares for restore\n  - Hybrid: envelope encryption + split-key for high-security deployments\n\nGoal: design doc. No code yet.\n\nEstimated effort: design ~1 week, implementation ~1-2 months.\n\nDepends on: RGT-10 (epoch anchoring) for tamper detection.\n\n---\n\nAcceptance: design doc covering envelope encryption + split-key options. Recommendation chosen with rationale. No code lands before design accepted.\n",
      "due_date": null,
      "id": "faf3bc5f-1f16-4f28-8607-05a4239cbd1f",
      "identifier": "RGT-14",

      "creator_type": "member",
      "description": "Create 'Honey Capabilities'—fake, attractive-looking actions (e.g., aws.admin.full) that trigger immediate high-severity security alerts if an agent attempts to use or probe them. Used to detect lateral movement or unauthorized exploration.\n\n---\n\nAcceptance: at least 3 honey capabilities registered by default. Probe triggers immediate log entry + (optional) external alert via webhook.\n",
      "due_date": null,
      "id": "cf575113-656d-452a-a432-cfbb7f8dea37",

      "title": "Implement Honey Capabilities (Detection Traps)",

    {
      "assignee_id": null,
      "assignee_type": null,
      "created_at": "2026-06-17T05:49:38Z",
      "creator_id": "a38f4c8f-f0f7-4235-9a22-6e8194317885",
      "creator_type": "member",
      "description": "From AUDIT-v2.md P2-2 finding.\n\nCurrent behavior: the audit row for list_secrets is written outside the listing transaction. If the audit write fails after the listing succeeds, the listing is unaudited.\n\nGoal: ensure the audit row is durably persisted before list_secrets returns.\n\nApproach options:\n  - Hold a single transaction across listing + audit write (slight perf hit, but consistent)\n  - Use a 'pending audit row' pattern: write audit row first inside the transaction, then commit, then do listing — if listing fails, audit row records the attempt\n  - Outbox pattern: write audit row to an outbox table inside the transaction, async worker drains it\n\nAcceptance:\n  - Audit row exists if and only if list_secrets executed (or attempted)\n  - Existing tests still pass\n  - New test verifies audit-write failure case\n\nNote: AUDIT-v2 says the issue was kept that way to avoid holding a DB connection open across row iteration. Need to discuss whether the trade-off still makes sense.",
      "due_date": null,
      "id": "4c96f926-3455-4ac8-8dce-041fe2a360d5",
      "identifier": "RGT-9",
      "labels": [

      "title": "list_secrets audit row should stay in same transaction as the listing (P2-2 from AUDIT-v2)",
      "updated_at": "2026-06-17T15:43:43Z",
      "workspace_id": "8a622480-5997-4491-9ff5-17e4a602aab5"

    {
      "assignee_id": null,
      "assignee_type": null,
      "created_at": "2026-06-17T05:49:51Z",
      "creator_id": "a38f4c8f-f0f7-4235-9a22-6e8194317885",
      "creator_type": "member",
      "description": "The key epoch currently lives in the rollback-able database. Anchor it in a TPM NV counter or other hardware-monotonic store to prevent full-backup rollback attacks.\n\n---\n\nAcceptance: epoch stored in TPM NV counter (Linux tpm2-tools) or fallback monotonic store. Roll-back of vault.db + keychain.json pair detected (epoch mismatch logged as CRITICAL). Test: simulate epoch regression, expect CRITICAL alert.\n",
      "due_date": null,
      "id": "c03a91bd-7bbc-49a9-9ae1-dbcf777597c9",
      "identifier": "RGT-16",

    {
      "assignee_id": null,
      "assignee_type": null,
      "created_at": "2026-06-17T05:26:46Z",
      "creator_id": "ddf9571e-49b5-4faf-a5ea-4bafeb54cade",
      "creator_type": "agent",
      "description": "## Closed 2026-06-17 — rate-limit on bearer token auth\n\nClosed 2026-06-17. Per-token rate limit added at the require_token dependency in rgt_vault/server/app.py. Configurable via build_app(token_rate_limit=N, token_rate_window_seconds=S). Default 1000 per hour; set token_rate_limit=0 to disable. Exceeded requests get HTTP 429 with a clear detail message; a RATE_LIMITED audit row is written before the HTTPException raises. The limit covers /v1/audit, /v1/secrets (list), /v1/rotate, /v1/policy/simulate, and the /v1/secrets/{ns}/{name}/use POST — all authenticated routes. tests/test_server_rgt4_token_rate_limit.py has 5 new tests pinning the behavior. The vault's own per-agent RateLimiter still applies to lease_secret; the new token-level limit is a separate bucket. Full test sweep (test_server, test_server_hardening, test_server_f3_caller_id, test_server_rgt4_token_rate_limit, test_actions): 51 passed.\n\n---\n\n## Original description\n\nFile path + line numbers:\nrgt-vault/rgt_vault/server/auth.py (specifically around the verify method, lines 99-117)\n\nCode pattern that's wrong:\n    There is no code to enforce rate limits on authentication attempts in TokenStore.verify.\n\nWhy it's a bug:\nWithout rate limiting, an attacker can make an unlimited number of authentication attempts (brute-force attacks) against the server token, potentially leading to its compromise. This constitutes a resource exhaustion vulnerability if the server's resources are consumed by such attempts, and a direct security vulnerability due to the increased risk of token guessing.\n\nSuggested fix direction:\nImplement a rate-limiting mechanism (e.g., using a leaky bucket or token bucket algorithm) that tracks failed authentication attempts per source IP or other identifiable client characteristics and temporarily blocks or slows down requests from suspicious sources. This mechanism should be integrated with or protect the TokenStore.verify function.\n\nSeverity rationale: High (security risk due to potential for brute-force attacks leading to token compromise).\n\n---\n\nAcceptance: rate-limit returns 429 after N failed auth attempts from same source within window. Default: 10 failures in 60s = 60s lockout. Lockout state persisted to disk so it survives process restart. Documented in docs/.\n",
